## VR HUD

How the game's 2D HUD is resized and placed in head-tracked frames (`runtime/src/ps2_video.c`, anchor maths in `runtime/src/ps2_xr.c`).

| Variable | Default | Values | Meaning | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ACZ_VR_HUD_SCALE` | `1.0` (ENHANCEMENTS HUD SIZE: `1.3`) | multiplier; `0` = off | HUD size relative to its original angular size (1 = pitch ladder matches the horizon) | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:14367`, `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:35559`, `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:35966` |
| `ACZ_VR_HUD_TAN_H` | `0.5625` | tangent | The game's own horizontal half-FOV tangent, the reference for HUD scaling | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:14369`, `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:35560`, `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:35967` |
| `ACZ_VR_HUD_TAN_V` | `0.4219` | tangent | The game's own vertical half-FOV tangent | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:14370`, `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:35561`, `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:35968` |
| `ACZ_VR_HUD_ANCHOR` | on | `0` | Keep the direction HUD (ladder, heading tape, aircraft symbol) on the aircraft's nose in world-locked frames | `runtime/src/ps2_xr.c:402` |
| `ACZ_VR_HUD_ANCHOR_BOX` | `0.42,0.62,0.45` | `"x,up,down"` (half-screen units) or `all` | Middle zone whose HUD draws count as direction HUD and get anchored; `all` anchors everything | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:35593`, `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:38086` |
| `ACZ_VR_HUD_COCKPIT_DIST` | `0.55` | metres; `0` = in front of the eyes | Cockpit frames: the rest of the HUD (radar, boxes, weapons) sits on a plane this far ahead of the eye point | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:14372`, `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:35629` |
| `ACZ_VR_MARKER` | on | `0` | Keep each target box's label (TGT, FRND...) with its box: no HUD scaling or anchoring, at infinity like the box, same reprojection (before: the label was moved 15-20 deg away) | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:36077` |
| `ACZ_VR_MARKER_SCALE` | `1` | factor | Scale target boxes, their depth-tested copies and their labels together about the box centre | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:35768` |
| `ACZ_VR_MARKER_RANGE` | `1` | `0` | (bug #316, 2026-11-05: "from afar it is a mess of all these squares ... a bit smaller, not tiny, so they do not cover the target") A target box's size is a curve of the target's range, on top of ACZ_VR_MARKER_SCALE: the box, its depth-tested copy, a far target's bracket frame and the red lock diamond; the text beside the box keeps its size and follows the box's edge. The range is the box's own: its depth-tested copy lies at the target's depth (calibrated against the units' places, see NOTES). `0` = the game's size, these draws byte for byte. The curve is our design: no published source says how the newer games size theirs. ACZ_DEBUG_MARKER=1 logs range and scale now and then | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:35793`, `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:35799` |
| `ACZ_VR_MARKER_RANGE_NEAR` | `1000` | metres | (bug #316) Up to this range a box has its full size | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:35801` |
| `ACZ_VR_MARKER_RANGE_FAR` | `8000` | metres | (bug #316) From this range on a box has ACZ_VR_MARKER_RANGE_FAR_SCALE; straight in log range between NEAR and FAR (the same factor per doubling) | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:35802` |
| `ACZ_VR_MARKER_RANGE_FAR_SCALE` | `0.5` | factor `0.35`..`1` | (bug #316) The size at FAR and beyond; below 0.35 it is held at 0.35, the legible floor (~1.7 deg in the headset at TARGET BOX SIZE 1X) | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:35803` |
| `ACZ_VR_MARKER_RANGE_AB` | off | `1` | [test] (bug #316) Odd game frames at the game's size, even ones by range: PS2_XR_DUMP_PAIR=2 dumps the two sizes of one target one game frame apart in one run | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:35808` |
| `ACZ_VR_MARKER_INFO` | on | `0` | (bug #135, 2026-10-27, M5: "again this box with the distance moves away from the aircraft") Everything the game writes beside a target box stays with the box: the selected target's name and distance (two HUD-green lines right of the box, above its top and below its bottom), a friendly's callsign / order lines in its blue (also right-aligned to the box's left edge; long callsigns were cut at 48 px) and the red diamond the game puts on a locked target's box (depth test ALWAYS at the target's depth, so taken for HUD) -- drawn where the game put them, at infinity like the box, scaled with ACZ_VR_MARKER_SCALE, hidden by the frames like it, instead of being scaled and anchored like the HUD (x 0.53 across / 0.40 up-down at HUD SIZE 1.3, towards the gun cross: M10 at 295 m with the box 21 deg above the view centre, the name / distance and the red lock diamond sat 16-17 deg below it). Info glyphs: at most 12 px, the box's texture, any colour, 48 px left .. 80 px right and 16 px above / below the box, straight after it in the draw order; a marker sprite: from the boxes' texture, 6-40 px, a depth between the radio texts' (~6e-8) and the HUD's (1). `0` = as before | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:35744` |
| `ACZ_VR_MARKER_FRAME` | on | `0` | (cv2_vis 2026-10-27, M7 13:50:40: "this square that moves should stay in the collimator, but it is not only in the collimator") The bracket frame the game draws around a far selected target's box (M7's CTRL TOWER at 60-100 km: ~48 x 45 PS2 px of plain lines with corner ticks, in the box's own colour and faint alpha, 75,255,55,22) stays with the box: drawn where the game put it, at infinity, scaled with ACZ_VR_MARKER_SCALE about the box's centre, hidden by the cockpit like the box. Before, it classified as HUD (untextured depth-ALWAYS lines in the HUD's middle zone), was scaled about the boresight (x 0.53 / 0.38 at HUD SIZE 1.3) and anchored to the aircraft, so it sat at about half the target's angle from the nose (7-13 deg below the nose in a 25 deg climb for a target 25 deg below), apart from the box, the name and the distance it frames on the PS2. Matched: untextured HUD-class lines in a box's exact colour and alpha within 32 PS2 px of a box of this or the last frame. `0` = as before | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:35739` |
| `ACZ_VR_MARKER_ROLL` | on | `0` | (hud_world 2026-10-28, bug #151, M7 22:55:26 "this ACC should not turn like that", M8 23:37:28 "the icons turn here too") Target boxes, their labels (TGT, FRND), names and distances, the red lock diamond, a far target's bracket frame and the bombs' / rockets' aiming ellipse lean with the aircraft, as on the PS2, whose screen is fixed to the aircraft: each is turned about its box's centre by the aircraft's roll as seen at that place (the angle between the head camera's up and the aircraft's up / right direction there, the same perspective as the anchored HUD). The game renders with the head's camera, roll included, and draws them upright on its image: with the head rolled 25 deg against the cockpit they stood 25 deg off the cockpit and the rest of the HUD, and turned against it with every head roll. Place, size and shape stay the game's; head-locked frames untouched. Sim: head roll 25 -> boxes and text turned 25.0 deg (ACZ_DEBUG_MARKER logs it). `0` = upright to the eye, as before | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:35848` |
| `ACZ_VR_MARKER_BLINK` | on | `0` | (hud_world 2026-10-28, bug #151, M7 22:51:18-24: CTRL TOWER's name, distance and frame small and off to one side every other tenth of a second) The game blinks some target boxes (a ground target under the RCL's ellipse: 10 fields drawn, 10 not) but writes their name / distance (and a far selected target's bracket frame) every field; without the box they followed the box drawn before them, the distance line (13-22 px below) and the frame went the HUD's way: x 0.53 / 0.40 towards the boresight and anchored. Now a HUD-green info glyph that continues the boxes' chain but not where the game writes a box's text (name 3 px right of the box, 9 px above its top; distance under its bottom) and starts a new line stands in for the missing 16 x 13 box (3 px left of it, 9 px below its top), which takes its text and frame like a drawn box. Sim (M7, RCL near CTRL TOWER, head swept +-12 deg and rolled 20): glyphs near a box sent to the HUD path 6,996 in 453 fields -> 0. `0` = as before | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:35761` |
| `ACZ_VR_MARKER_AIM` | on | `0` | (hud_world 2026-10-28, bug #152, M7 A-10A 23:20:58 "it must show exactly where the bomb hits") The bombs' and rocket pods' aiming ellipse (HUD atlas cell s 2..39, t 125..162, 36 x 31 PS2 px) is placed by the game where its camera -- the head-turned one in VR -- sees the impact point (rockets: the boresight); the bombs' fall line (5 dashes, untextured lines with the FST bit, right after the ellipse) runs from the game's screen centre to the ellipse's edge. Both were HUD-scaled about the boresight and anchored: the ellipse 5.6 deg off the impact direction with the head straight, 14.2 / 17.2 deg with the head turned (yaw 20 pitch -10 / yaw -15 pitch -25 roll 15; M7 FAEB dive). Now the ellipse stays at the game's place (at infinity, turning with the world between game frames), at the HUD's size (HUD SIZE about its own centre) and with the aircraft's lean (ACZ_VR_MARKER_ROLL), hidden by the cockpit like a box: 0.00 / 0.26 / 0.08 deg off; the fall line runs from the aircraft's boresight (the HUD's gun cross) to that ellipse's edge, its dashes spread over the new length. `0` = both drawn as HUD, as before | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:35914` |
| `ACZ_VR_MARKER_AIM_LINE` | on | `0` | (hud_place 2026-10-31, bug #256, M13 F-117A 11:45:02 "with the head's movement the bomb's trajectory line moves; it should not be so", 11:46:28 "something peeks through ahead, some sprite") The guided bombs' (GPB) fall line: 5-12 untextured dashes without the FST bit (the UGB / FAEB line's 5 have it), drawn right after the aiming ellipse in its colour, from the round symbol at the game's screen centre (the head's forward in VR; it slides in from the screen's edge when the line appears) to the ellipse's edge. They took the HUD's way: scaled about the boresight, those in the HUD's middle zone anchored to the aircraft, the rest on the cockpit plane 0.55 m ahead (M13, head yaw 12 pitch -6: 4 of 12 dashes at 0.55 m, the line in pieces outside the combiner and over the HUD body, sliding with every head turn). Now they are recognised as the fall line (the run of lines right after the ellipse, in its colour, on one straight line through its centre) and drawn as ACZ_VR_MARKER_AIM draws it: from where the HUD shows that round symbol (at rest the gun cross) to the ellipse, which stays 40-43 game px under the boresight at every head pose -- the line now holds still with the head at yaw 12 / pitch -6, yaw -10 / pitch 4, yaw 15, pitch 10 and roll 25 (ev/gpb_*); it never runs past the gun cross or into the ellipse. `0` = the GPB dashes on the HUD path as before (the FST dashes keep ACZ_VR_MARKER_AIM) | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:35935` |
| `ACZ_DEBUG_MARKER` | off | `1`, `2` | [debug] Log the box/label associations (with each box's texels, filter and blend; the first info glyph of each box, the HUD-class box copies kept with their box) and, every 3000 draws, how many symbols at infinity come before the cockpit model / the edge AA pass; `2` also every HUD draw from a box's texture within 96 px of it that stays HUD (`not label`) | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:28617`, `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:35827`, `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:35893` (+1 more) |
| `ACZ_VR_MARKER_SHARP` | `3` | `0`, `1`, `2` | How target boxes draw their texels (labels and the rest of the HUD keep the smooth replacement): `3` = the texels as the line drawing they are (gs.frag marker_lines, bug #98: the ground targets' octagons and cube icons were "too pixelated" in `2` -- their diagonal outlines are single texels touching at the corners, drawn as staircases of beads): every lit texel a node, 8-adjacent nodes joined by straight segments (a diagonal chain a straight line 0.8 texel wide, a dot a disc, a dash a capsule), the game's anti-aliasing fringe (a texel next to one over 3.3x as bright) left out, no filled corners, one-pixel anti-aliased edge; square boxes look as in `2`; `2` = each original texel as a crisp shape at the exact sub-pixel place the sprite maps it to -- a one-pixel anti-aliased edge, lit neighbours joined, outer corners rounded (a lone dot a disc, a dash a capsule), every texel row kept (the game squashes the 16 x 16 cell into 16 x 13 pixels and its point filter drops rows) and smooth motion instead of whole-game-pixel steps (bug #67: `1` looked pixelated in the headset at ~8 display pixels per texel); needs the boxes' own blend by the source alpha, else `1`; `1` = flat, one colour per original texel on the game's pixel grid (bug #48, 2026-10-22); `0` = the 4x Real-ESRGAN atlas (blurred beads) | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:30974`, `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:38610` |
| `ACZ_VR_MARKER_ROUND` | on | `0` | With ACZ_VR_MARKER_SHARP=2: the cells' outer corners rounded (half a texel); `0` = square cells with anti-aliased edges | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:38612` |
| `ACZ_VR_MARKER_AB` | off | frames N | [test] The target boxes as line drawings (ACZ_VR_MARKER_SHARP=3) and as shapes (`2`) in alternate blocks of N frames; the mean GPU time per frame of each logged every 40 blocks (`marker-ab:`); `PS2_PERF_AB=1 PS2_PERF_AB_FLIP=markerlines` gives the exact same-frame image A/B | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:14727` |
| `ACZ_VR_HUD_OCCLUDE` | on | `0` | Frames with the 3D cockpit model: target boxes, their labels and the collimated middle of the HUD (gun cross, and ladder / heading tape where shown) are drawn only where no part of the model is in front -- seen through the canopy and the HUD glass, cut by frames, the HUD body and the glare shield (depth bounds, like the sun); `0` draws them over the model as before | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:295`, `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:308`, `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:36392` (+1 more) |
| `ACZ_VR_HUD_GHOST` | `0` | `0`..`1` | Opacity of a faint copy of hidden target boxes and labels drawn over the cockpit model (try 0.25-0.35); `0` = hidden completely | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:36393` |
| `ACZ_VR_HUD_ARROW` | `head` | `head`, `aircraft`, `cockpit` | The target arrow (runs along the HUD's edge, pointing at the selected target when it is out of view; the game computes it relative to the head): `head` = fixed to the head like a helmet-display cue, at infinity; `aircraft` = anchored to the aircraft like the collimated HUD; `cockpit` = on the cockpit plane 0.55 m ahead (the old behaviour) | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:36396` |
| `ACZ_VR_HUD_ARROW_GHOST` | `1` | `0`..`1` | `1` = the arrow is drawn in full everywhere (a helmet cue); below 1 it is hidden by the cockpit model like the boxes, with a copy at this opacity over the model | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:36398` |
| `ACZ_DEBUG_HUDOCC` | off | N | [debug] Log the target arrow's lines (PS2 pixels) every N fields, and whether the old side band (ACZ_VR_HUD_ARROW_FIND=band) would have found them | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:36403` |
| `ACZ_VR_HUD_ARROW_RING` | `0.45` | `0.05`..`1` | The target arrow (head / aircraft modes) moved towards the middle of the view by this factor, same shape and direction: ~16 deg sideways / ~10-13 deg up and down at HUD scale 1.3 instead of 25-34 deg at the edge of the view (bugs #43/#52: "an arrow somewhere at the side, very far away"); `1` = the game's place | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:36400` |
| `ACZ_VR_HUD_ARROW_FIND` | `ring` | `band` | How the target arrow's three short lines are recognised: `ring` = anywhere on the game's ring around the HUD's middle (an ellipse of ~225 x 190 PS2 px, 0.85-1.15 of it; lines of at most 12 px drawn with the full screen's scissor), before the HUD's blocks; `band` = the old test after the blocks: only the side / top / bottom bands, so in the four diagonal sectors the arrow stayed a plain HUD draw out at the game's place on the cockpit plane, and down-right the weapon block took it onto the weapons screen (bug #70, F-14D: "the arrows drive to the screen and behave strangely") | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:36438`, `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:38300` |
| `ACZ_VR_HUD_ARROW_LONG` | on | `0` | (hud_world 2026-10-28, bug #153, M8 23:39:52 "this arrow of mine moves; strange") The target arrow is a triangle whose two long sides reach 13-14 PS2 px when it points left or right (M7: 14 x 3, 1 x 9, 13 x 6 px at the left edge); with ACZ_VR_HUD_ARROW_FIND=ring's 12-px limit only its short side was taken for the arrow (fixed to the head, pulled towards the middle) and the long sides stayed HUD lines (scaled and anchored to the aircraft): the arrow fell apart into a stroke and a wedge 90 PS2 px apart (M7, head 12 deg off) that slid against each other with every head turn. Now slanted lines (both sides of their box >= 2 px) up to 16 px on the ring count too; the axis-aligned 16-17 px edges of the weapon rows on that ring still do not. `0` = the 12-px test alone | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:36458` |
| `ACZ_VR_HUD_WING` | `side` | `0`, `x,y` | Frames with the 3D cockpit model: the wingman commands (D-pad icon, COVER bar, dashed line; the game's 132..247 x 386..422, -20..-2 deg x -22..-26 deg at HUD scale 1.3, just above or over the middle screens) move away from the cockpit's screens (bug #69, F-14D: "move the COVER icon aside or lower"; the J35J's scope): with the radar on a screen they take the radar's corner of the HUD (left edge x 16, bottom left), stepping up 8 px at a time while within ~1.5 deg of a screen seen from the design eye; with the radar in the HUD they stay unless near a screen (then up); logged as `hud-wing:` per model. Since 2026-10-31 (ck_screens, bugs #257 / #259: the Su-47's block sat on its left MFD's bezel and the airspeed dial) the screens count with their bezels and a head off the design eye, and the live dials count too (ACZ_VR_HUD_WING_BEZEL / _HEAD / _DIALS: the first of screens + bezels + dials, screens + bezels, the lit faces that leaves a place wins), the D-pad menu moves with the block where it fits (ACZ_VR_HUD_WING_MENU), a model can have its own place (ACZ_VR_HUD_WING_MODEL: the FALKEN) and a wingman display takes the block off the HUD (ACZ_VR_MFD `wingman`). `0` = the game's place; `x,y` = a fixed shift in the game's pixels | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:14319`, `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:34865` |
| `ACZ_VR_HUD_WEAPONS` | `side` | `0`, `x,y` | Frames with the 3D cockpit model, a cockpit with the radar on a screen but no weapon screen (bug #97): where the weapon block's place (387..491 x 288..425, bottom right) covers one of the model's screens seen from the design eye -- the MiG-29A's IPV now showing the radar lay behind its silhouette and rows -- it moves to the radar's freed corner (left edge x 14), as low as it fits clear of the screens, the moved wingman commands and the HUD's SPEED box, its bracket and SCORE where those still show (8 px steps; MiG-29A and A-10A -373,-48; the Su-27 keeps SPEED in the HUD: no place, the block stays); logged as `hud-weapons:`. `0` = the game's place; `x,y` = a fixed shift in the game's pixels | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:14324` |
| `ACZ_VR_HUD_WING_BEZEL` | `0.2` | fraction `0`..`2` | (ck_screens 2026-10-31, bugs #257 / #259; the Su-47 11:10:28 "this cover covers a screen, maybe raise it a bit") ACZ_VR_HUD_WING `side`: each screen's box (its lit face seen from the design eye, cpit.scr_tan) grows by this fraction of its own width / height on each side for the display's bezel and keys before the 10 px margin. Su-47: its left MFD's face top was 15 px under the block's bottom (5 px after the margin), the bezel and keys ~20 px more. `0` = the lit faces only, as before | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:14328` |
| `ACZ_VR_HUD_WING_HEAD` | `0.04` | metres `0`..`0.5` | (ck_screens 2026-10-31) ACZ_VR_HUD_WING `side`: each screen's box also grows by the parallax between the HUD's plane (ACZ_VR_HUD_COCKPIT_DIST / _MODEL) and the screen's mean depth for a head this far above, below or beside the design eye: head x abs(1 / plane - 1 / depth) (0.55 m plane, 0.62 m Su-47 MFD: 0.8 mrad, ~3 px; 0.88 m Su-34 lower displays: ~11 px). `0` = from the design eye only | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:14330` |

Su-47: -116,0 (on the left MFD's bezel and the airspeed dial) -> -116,-64 (over the coaming, clear of the dials; head 4 cm up still clear). Offline replica over all 44 cockpit files (a private script): 15 move up by 8-40 px (F-15C -56 -> -72, F-15E / both S/MTD -64 -> -88, F-16C -24 -> -48, F-16XL -56 -> -72, EA-18G -48 -> -72, M2000D -32 -> -56, Rafale 0 -> -24, Su-37 0 -> -32, Su-47 0 -> -64, X-02 0 -> -24, MiG-29A 0 -> -8, Gripen -56 -> -96 / FlightGear Gripen 0 -> -32; the Gripen block now goes onto its data display), the rest keep their place. `0` = screens only | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:14332` |
| `ACZ_VR_HUD_SCISSOR_CLIP` | on | `0` | (bug #305, 2026-11-05, M4 "this map moves with the head; when the green jammer appears it rotates with the head") A flat HUD draw that overflows its own scissor (the radar's window) is cut to it in the game's pixels before it is placed. On the cockpit plane one scissor serves both eyes' shifted copies, so it was widened by the eye shift: the radar jamming's green rings (and, faintly, the radar's own range rings) showed past the box, on the right in the right eye, on the left in the left eye, more with a head turn. Draws inside their scissor are untouched. `0` = as before | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:38207` |
| `ACZ_VR_HUD_WING_MENU` | on | `0` | (ck_screens 2026-10-31, bug #257: "the map and the command list") The wingman commands' D-pad menu belongs to their block: while a D-pad direction is held the game draws, instead of the idle block (132..247 x 386..421), a cross of commands under the HUD's middle -- the D-pad 246..266 x 360..401, ATK 224..288 x 345..364, CVR 224..288 x 397..416, DSP 185..249 x 371..390, SPW 278..342 x 371..390 and after DSP / SPW a sub-list 145..209 x 349..412 (M1, ACZ_DEBUG_MFD_FIELD). The block's place grows to 128..346 x 340..428 (target box parts never count); a field with a wingman draw outside the idle place is a menu field (its draws MFD_WMENU, their moved copies MFD_WMENU_ALT). The menu shows on a wingman display, and in the HUD moves with the moved block where its box then lies inside the HUD clear of the screens with their bezels, a moved weapon block and SPEED / its bracket / SCORE (logged `hud-wing: ... their D-pad menu moves with them` / `stays`). Before, the menu stayed at the game's place over the middle screens and the sub-list's third entry jumped to the moved block's corner. `0` = the idle place only, as before | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:1288` |

Each draw of a head-tracked frame carries its HUD layer (`rcl_layer`; a draw of one layer is never merged into another's). The frame's own pass is unchanged but for one copy: right before the first draw of a hidden layer -- after our passes before the HUD (cockpit model, eye adaptation, edge AA, trails, clouds) -- the scene's right-eye layer and its depth (inside the band the window shows, +16 px at 4x) go into a private target; after the eye copies the draws that followed, minus the hidden ones (the kept HUD, the sun's overlays, screen passes, the game's composite into the displayed target), are drawn again into it and a private displayed target, which the window presents, with the G veil / head fade as in the headset. The headset's targets and the frame's host data are never written by it. Sim (M7 Gripen, user settings): the snapshot at 89-94 % of the list, ~70-160 draws left out and ~16 drawn again a frame; the window image equals the headset's right image to the bit except the hidden HUD; GPU (exclusive 4x, user settings, governor off, ACZ_REC_CLEAN_AB=30): M2 F-15C +0.163 ms per frame (5 blocks +0.163..+0.170; GPU ~3.8 ms), M7 Gripen +0.165 (+0.159..+0.172): the snapshot ~0.035, the tail ~0.12 (ACZ_REC_CLEAN_TEST); ~0.29 ms on the headset (x1.75). CPU (renderer): the tail recorded in 0.02-0.06 ms a frame, the snapshot 0.005. VRAM ~117 MB at 4x (two private targets). Recognised by at least ACZ_REC_CLEAN_STATIC_MIN HUD-coloured 2D sprites in the field while the game flies (the flight camera updated within 30 fields). Before, the 2026-11-01 KEEP=none recordings showed the whole HUD at each mission start (58-60 frames of the cockpit view with MISSION START and the FPS counter between "start view: cockpit" and the engagement: M1 / M3 / M6 / M11 / M18), at the stage-load resume holds (5 frames: M15 MISSION UPDATE, M18 MISSION START) and in every pause menu; 2026-11-02 M18 (acz_cine_2026-11-02_10-46-08, 627-650 s) flipped between the view and the frame's own image while frames went without the head for ~3 s. The game's cinematics (intro flybys, event cameras such as the take-off clearance, cutscenes, the crash replay) stay as the game draws them, with their subtitles and "PRESS START BUTTON TO SKIP" (under the threshold); menus without the flight HUD too. | `ACZ_REC_CINE` | off (`play_zero_desktop.sh record cine`: `1`) | `1` | The recording's cinematic view (wish #245, rec_cine 2026-10-30; VR stereo with multiview only): the desktop window -- what OBS records -- shows a view of its own instead of an eye: one rectilinear 16:9 camera at ACZ_REC_CINE_FOV, no lens corners, crop, eye masks or foveated shading, the HUD as ACZ_REC_CLEAN_KEEP leaves it (default: the gunsight only), steadied (ACZ_REC_CINE_SMOOTH): it rides the cockpit exactly (the aircraft's camera of the game frame), only the head's own turn and position in the cockpit are smoothed, and it is pulled towards the head whenever its corners would leave what the frame drew (ACZ_REC_CINE_MARGIN). Once per game frame, after the headset's eye copies, the frame's draw list is recorded again (`cine_run`) into private targets of the slots it needs (the scene, the displayed target, what reads them: Zero's sun chain 13-16) from that camera: the game's world, HUD and sun draws re-projected by depth (gs.vert / mesh.vert push bit 131072; near draws and the HUD from their eye shift), the 3D cockpit with its screens, mirrors (the frame's map) and gloves, our far clouds (and the near field turned from the frame's buffer: ACZ_REC_CINE_NEAR), trails, edge AA, the veils, eye adaptation drawn from it (ps2_xr_cine_override, the calling thread only). The headset's images are made before it and its targets, host data and per-frame state are not written (a re-draw of the frame after it: PS2_PERF_AB=3). The window is ACZ_REC_CINE_WINDOW pixels, the view's rectangle resampled into it (ACZ_REC_CINE_FILTER); frames without the head (menus, movies, the static screen) show the window as without it, but the flight HUD on the static screen and held stereo frames do not (ACZ_REC_CLEAN_STATIC). Sim (exclusive 4x, user settings, governor off, ACZ_REC_CINE_AB, M1 F-5E in the cockpit; the simulator's ~60 Hz puts the view in the game frames): +0.909 / +0.866 ms per frame that draws it (two runs; game frames 3.40 / 3.37 -> 4.31 / 4.23 ms), 70 deg +0.768; both views and no cull (before ACZ_REC_CINE_MONO / _CULL) +1.44. On the headset (x1.75) ~+1.55 ms on the frame between game frames that draws it, the game frames untouched. CPU (renderer) 0.44-0.46 ms a view; VRAM 336 MB at 4x (six private 2048x1792 two-layer targets) + their snapshots. The view's own pixel density: ACZ_REC_CINE_SCALE (default the eyes'). Off: nothing changes | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:29386` |
| `ACZ_REC_CINE_FOV` | `85` | degrees across, `30`-`100` | ACZ_REC_CINE: the view's horizontal field of view (16:9: 85 -> 54.5 deg high). Spectator cameras of VR games and VR video makers use 80-90 (LIV 50 / 70 / 90, the Beat Saber camera mod 90, VTOL VR 60); 60-70 for tele inserts. Wider than the frame allows (Quest 3: 108 x 109 deg, less the margin) it is narrowed with a log line. At 4x the view's rectangle is 1364 x 664 target pixels (the PS2 pixels are not square): every pixel of the eyes' density at that angle (744 px per tangent across, 627 down) | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:29412` |
| `ACZ_REC_CINE_SMOOTH` | `0.09,0.5,0.07` | half-lives in seconds: yaw / pitch, roll, position (`0` = none) | ACZ_REC_CINE: how the head's own motion in the cockpit is smoothed -- its yaw (about the cockpit's up), pitch and roll each move 1 - 2^(-dt / half-life) of the way to the head per frame (the yaw the short way round; near straight up the yaw holds), the position likewise. Defaults: Half-Life: Alyx's spectator camera (vr_camera_decay_yaw_pitch_halflife 0.09, roll 0.5), position ~0.1 s as other VR spectator cameras. The aircraft's own motion is never lagged (the camera rides the game frame's aircraft camera). Sim, 1.5 deg head jitter (PS2_XR_TEST_HEAD_JITTER): M1 4x, frame-to-frame steps (RMS) head -> camera: yaw 0.231 -> 0.063 deg, pitch 0.249 -> 0.067, roll 0.130 -> 0.007, position 1.07 -> 0.32 mm; a scripted 60 deg turn reaches 90 % 67 ms after the head; during turns the camera is 3.2 deg (max 10.7) off the head, pulled in on 39 of 400 frames (a private script) | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:29419` |
| `ACZ_REC_CINE_ROLL` | `smooth` | `smooth`, `lock` | ACZ_REC_CINE: the head's roll smoothed (its half-life, ACZ_REC_CINE_SMOOTH) or `lock`ed: no head roll at all, the view level with the cockpit (the aircraft's bank stays) | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:29424` |
| `ACZ_REC_CINE_PITCH` | `0` | degrees, `-20`-`20` | ACZ_REC_CINE: the view tilted up (+) or down (-) from the smoothed head, about its own x axis: more sky and less instrument panel for flat-screen framing (0: where the pilot looks) | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:29429` |
| `ACZ_REC_CINE_MARGIN` | `1` | degrees | ACZ_REC_CINE: how far inside the frame's field of view (what the game drew for the head) the view's corners and edges must stay; the smoothed camera is pulled towards the head as far as needed (a fast head turn is followed at about the margin's distance: 85 deg across leaves ~10.5 deg sideways, ~26 deg up / down) | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:29425` |
| `ACZ_REC_CINE_WINDOW` | `2560x1440` (with ACZ_REC_CINE) | `WxH`, `full`, `keep` | ACZ_REC_CINE: the game window's size in pixels while the view is on -- the OBS canvas of "ACZ VR cine" (obs_setup.py ACZ_REC_CINE_RES); not saved to ac5_settings.ini. `full` = borderless on the window's monitor at its desktop size (3840x2160 here: the screen is covered), `keep` = the configured window. Logged with the desktop's mode and scale (`rec-cine: window`) | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:29395` |
| `ACZ_REC_CINE_FILTER` | `bicubic` | `bicubic`, `bilinear` | ACZ_REC_CINE: how the window resamples the view's rectangle (present.frag mode 3: Catmull-Rom in nine bilinear taps, crisper edges than bilinear when upscaling 1364 x 664 to 2560 x 1440; a view drawn at more pixels than the window, ACZ_REC_CINE_SCALE, is sampled down instead: up to 4 x 4 bilinear taps averaged) | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:29427` |
| `ACZ_REC_CINE_SCALE` | `1` (the eyes' density; a recording's height) | `1`; a multiple below 100 (`1.5`, `2`, ...); `1080`, `1440`, `2160` or `4k` | ACZ_REC_CINE: the view's own pixel density (bug #245, rec_cine2 2026-10-30). `1`: drawn at the eyes' upscale (PS2_UPSCALE), so at 4x / 85 deg the view's rectangle has 1364 x 664 pixels, resampled up to the window (as before). A height: the upscale S whose 16:9 rectangle holds about that many pixels, chosen at the first camera (4x / 85 deg: 1080 -> 6x, 2045 x 997; 1440 -> 8x, 2727 x 1329; 2160 -> 12x, 4090 x 1993; a narrower ACZ_REC_CINE_FOV takes a larger S for the same pixels); a multiple: S = it x PS2_UPSCALE; at most 16 and the device's image size; no more than the eyes' = the eyes'. The pass then records at S (rt_scale = S while it runs; every other target keeps its own scale): private targets at S for the scene, the displayed target, the depth their draws use and any slot laid about 1:1 onto them (cine_plan `big`; Zero's glow / blur chain stays at the frame's scale), the cockpit's 4x MSAA target and the canopy's world window-sized (a viewport moved by the window clip's corner; cockpit_comp.frag push `org`), the effects' depth snapshot the view's own, our near clouds from the frame's buffer through the two densities' homography; the window samples the rectangle down (present.frag downsample). Same frame at both densities (ACZ_REC_CINE_DUMP_PAIR; a private script): instrument lettering, dials, the canopy frame's edges and the canopy drops resolve (mean |Laplacian| of the window x1.4-1.6, of the instrument panel x1.8-2.6), the 2006 terrain and cloud textures change little. Cost (sim, exclusive 4x, governor off, ACZ_REC_CINE_AB, M1 F-5E in the cockpit, per game frame that draws the view): `1` (1364 x 664, 0.9 Mpx) +0.94 ms; `1080` (6x, 2045 x 997, 2.0 Mpx) +1.42 (median +1.46); `1440` (8x, 2727 x 1329, 3.6 Mpx) +2.10 (+2.23); `2.25` (9x, 3068 x 1495: 1440 in both axes, 4.6 Mpx) +2.50 (+2.64); `2160` (12x, 4090 x 1993, 8.2 Mpx) +3.93 -- about 0.55 ms + 0.41 ms per million pixels of the view (the frame itself 3.45-3.51 ms; two earlier runs with the GPU shared, frame 5.0-5.3 ms, gave +3.06 / +3.30 for 1080 / 1440); a narrower FOV does not make a native height cheaper (the same pixels); renderer CPU 0.49 -> 0.57-0.65 ms a view. VRAM of the view (images made at its first frames): 448 MB at 1, 863 MB at 1080, 1.28 GB at 1440, 2.5 GB at 2160. Headset (x1.75, the in-between frame at 120 Hz, ~5.5 ms of its own): +1.65 ms at `1` (~7.2 ms: fits 8.33), +2.5 at 1080 (~8.0: no margin), +3.7-3.9 at 1440 (~9.3: over), +6.9 at 2160. At 90 Hz (the Meta Quest Link app's refresh rate; ps2_xr.c does not choose one) smooth motion draws one frame between game frames for every second game frame, so the view goes into the game frames (ACZ_REC_CINE_WHEN): ~5.5-5.7 ms own of 11.1 -> 1080 ~8.1, 1440 ~9.3-9.6 (margin ~1.5 ms), 9x ~10.1, 2160 ~12.5 (72 Hz, 13.9 ms). Hence the default stays `1`; `record cine 1440` with the Link app at 90 Hz for a native 1440p master. The headset's image is unchanged (the pass after the eye copies; ACZ_REC_CINE_VERIFY at 1440: 40 passes, 0 changed); off and `1`: the same as before | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:29445` |
| `ACZ_REC_CINE_WHEN` | `between` | `between`, `game` | ACZ_REC_CINE: the view of a game frame is drawn in the frame drawn between it and the next game frame while smooth motion draws those (the headset's game frames, the heavier ones, keep their time; the window shows it from then on), else in the game frame itself; `game` = always in the game frame. `play_zero_desktop.sh record cine` sets `game` with the frame-exact recording (ACZ_REC_EXACT, bug #264): with `between` a game frame not followed by an in-between frame never had its view drawn (the 10-31 smooth sessions: 0-145 of 300 game frames per 5 s had none on the headset) and repeated the previous one in the recording | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:29432` |
| `ACZ_REC_CINE_MONO` | on | `0` | ACZ_REC_CINE: the view is drawn in layer 0 only: in its pass the game's draws, meshes and the 3D cockpit put view 1's primitives off screen (gs.vert / mesh.vert push bit 262144, cockpit.vert misc.w 2): no fragments for the second, unused layer; `0` = both views as the eyes (the same image twice). With ACZ_REC_CINE_CULL: +0.89 instead of +1.44 ms a view (sim 4x M1) | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:29428` |
| `ACZ_REC_CINE_CULL` | on | `0` | ACZ_REC_CINE: the scene's world and sun draws wholly outside the view's box seen from the frame's camera (+8 PS2 px) are left out of its pass (native meshes by their screen box, GS draws of up to 4096 vertices by their vertices). M1: 39-48 % of the draws left out (85 / 70 deg) | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:29433` |
| `ACZ_REC_CINE_NEAR` | on | `0` | ACZ_REC_CINE: our near clouds (ACZ_VR_CLOUDS with the near field) in the view: the frame's near buffer (marched for the head) composited through a homography of the head's turn against the view's (cloud_comp.frag near_px; the eye's few cm left out). `0` = only the far clouds in the view | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:29434` |
| `ACZ_REC_CINE_AB` | off | N frames | [test] ACZ_REC_CINE: blocks of N frames of the kind that draws the view (ACZ_REC_CINE_WHEN) with and without it (the "off" blocks show the frame's own image in the window); `rec-cine-ab:` the mean GPU ms of each every 40 N frames (a block's first 4 not counted) | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:29452` |
| `ACZ_REC_CINE_AFTER` | on | `0` | (perf_judder 2026-10-31, bug #255) ACZ_REC_CINE: a frame that draws the view gives the headset its images first -- the part of the frame recorded so far (the scene, our passes, the eye copies, the headset depth, the quit prompt's layer) goes to the GPU, the eye images are released and the XR frame ended, and only then is the view recorded; the view, the window and the rest follow as the frame's next part (one more early submission). The same commands in the same order on one queue, a barrier over all commands between the parts: the same images in the headset and in the window. Before, the headset's frame waited for the view's recording on the renderer and for its GPU work, and its last part for the window's swapchain image. Sim 4x, Rafale M12, in-run A/B (ACZ_REC_CINE_AFTER_AB=30, 18 pairs of 5-s windows): the headset's images ready 6.68 -> 4.97 ms after the recording start (-1.71 +- 0.04), the frame's own end 6.68 -> 7.04 (xrEndFrame between the parts; GPU idle, not GPU work). The view itself costs +1.55 (F-35C M14) to +1.72 ms (MiG-31 M11) of GPU a frame that draws it (ACZ_REC_CINE_AB; ~1.75x on the headset). Not in eye-dump frames that draw the frame again after the view (PS2_PERF_AB, PS2_XR_DUMP_NOCLOUD) nor with held parts (PS2_PERF_EARLY_DEFER). `0` = as before | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:30011` |
| `ACZ_REC_CINE_AFTER_AB` | off | N frames | [test] ACZ_REC_CINE_AFTER on and off in blocks of N frames that draw the view; the 5-s line `gpu-speed: frames that draw the cinematic view with / without ACZ_REC_CINE_AFTER` gives each arm's latency to the headset's images (recording start after xrWaitFrame -> the GPU end of their part), how many were over the headset's period, and the frame's own end (tools/perf/judder_report.py prints the means) | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:30012` |
| `ACZ_REC_EXACT` | off (`play_zero_desktop.sh record cine`: `1`; `record cine noexact`: `0`) | `1` | The frame-exact recording (bug #264, rec_pace 2026-11-02): the runtime encodes the window itself instead of OBS sampling it. OBS's game capture copied the window at its presents (at most one per 16.67 ms of its own clock, `limit_framerate`) and its video thread took the newest copy at its own 60 Hz ticks; the window presents in the XR frame that picks a game frame up, on the headset's vsync, which drifts against the field clock: in phases the pickups alternate 1 / 3 refreshes at 120 Hz (8 / 25 ms) and the recordings repeated (and lost) about one game frame per 1-refresh step -- 8-20 a second (M18 11-01, nosmooth: repeats per 5 s = 0.86 x the 1-refresh steps + 7.3, r = 0.81 over 68 moving 5-s windows: 1.3 a second without such steps, 3.5 with, up to 13; OBS itself lagged 3 of 23 250 frames); in smooth mode the views of game frames without a following in-between frame were never drawn (ACZ_REC_CINE_WHEN=between; 10-31 smooth session: repeats per 5 s = 0.36 x the game frames without an in-between frame + 23.5, r = 0.57; 6.0 a second in all). Now, in the frame that shows a game frame's content in the window for the first time (the frame that drew the cinematic view, or a game frame showing its own image: menus, movies), the presented window (with the F4 overlay) is blitted into an RGBA8 image of the recording's size and converted to NV12 BT.709 limited (rx_nv12.comp) into a host buffer (ACZ_REC_EXACT_BUFFERS), tagged with the list's field on the real-time field count (the guest's count + the fields the clock dropped); a writer thread (ps2_recexact.c) sends each once, in field order, to ffmpeg (ACZ_REC_EXACT_FFMPEG) through a named pipe as raw NV12 at the field rate -- a field without an image of its own (the renderer skipped the list, a lost capture) repeats the previous image, counted -- and the game's own mix (the audio callback's chunks, from the video's first frame on the monotonic clock; 1 sample in 200 repeated or left out while more than 10 ms off the clock, silence when no audio comes) through a second pipe; ffmpeg writes ACZ_REC_EXACT_OUT (x264 as OBS's cine master, the game as track "Game"), pinned to the efficiency cores. The file plays in real time whatever the headset or the desktop does; the headset's images and timing are untouched (the capture is in the window's part of the frame, after the headset's). Log: `rec-exact:` lines (frames written, fields repeated, captures lost, renderer waits, audio corrections; every 30 s and at the end; GPU per capture every 1800 captures). Sim (4x, M1 F-5E, 2560x1440 window, exclusive, governor off): GPU per capture 0.62-0.67 ms (blit 0.025, NV12 0.028, the 5.3 MB copy to host memory 0.56-0.62: bus-bound, in the window's part after the headset's images, so the headset's image of that frame is not later; it can push the next frame's GPU start); 0 renderer waits and 0 lost captures over 75 s with a stand-in for OBS's light encode on the same E-cores; the writer's pipe write 1.4-1.7 ms a frame; the game track is the mix sample for sample (against PS2_AUDIO_WAV: one constant lag, 0.00 ms spread over 44 s; 0 clock corrections); in play no field repeated by the recorder (the remaining repeats are the game's own: a 126 ms hitch, the R3 switch, the crash replay). VRAM +20 MB (RGBA8 image + the NV12 buffer), host memory +127 MB | `runtime/src/ps2_recexact.c:79` |
| `ACZ_REC_EXACT_OUT` | `out/rec_exact_<date>_<time>.mkv` (`record cine`: `~/Videos/ACZ_playtest/acz_cine_<date>_<time>.mkv`) | file path (`/` or `\`) | ACZ_REC_EXACT: the recording; ffmpeg's messages go to `<file>.ffmpeg.log` | `runtime/src/ps2_recexact.c:87` |
| `ACZ_REC_EXACT_FFMPEG` | `ffmpeg.exe` (on PATH) | file path | ACZ_REC_EXACT: the encoder (needs libx264, or hevc_nvenc for ACZ_REC_EXACT_ENCODER=nvenc) | `runtime/src/ps2_recexact.c:85` |
| `ACZ_REC_EXACT_ENCODER` | `x264` | `x264`, `nvenc` | ACZ_REC_EXACT: x264 on the CPU (as OBS's cine profile; bug #104) or NVENC HEVC constant QP, which Quest Link shares (~20 lost Link frames a second when OBS recorded with it) | `runtime/src/ps2_recexact.c:175` |
| `ACZ_REC_EXACT_CRF` | `16` (nvenc: `17` = its QP) | number | ACZ_REC_EXACT: x264 CRF (near-lossless master, as ACZ_REC_CINE_CRF) or the NVENC QP | `runtime/src/ps2_recexact.c:177` |
| `ACZ_REC_EXACT_PRESET` | `superfast` (nvenc: `p4`) | x264 / NVENC preset | ACZ_REC_EXACT: the encoder's preset. superfast CRF 16 at 2560x1440 60 fps needs ~70 % of the 8 E-cores (87 fps capacity there, ACZ_REC_CINE_PRESET's measurement); OBS's light reference profile (ACZ_REC_CINE_REF_RES) is ~1/6 of that | `runtime/src/ps2_recexact.c:178` |
| `ACZ_REC_EXACT_ARGS` | none | ffmpeg output options | [expert] ACZ_REC_EXACT: more options for ffmpeg's output (after the codec options, before the file name) | `runtime/src/ps2_recexact.c:182` |
| `ACZ_REC_EXACT_AFFINITY` | `auto` (the efficiency cores: logical processors 16-23 here) | `auto`, hex mask, `0` (Windows places it) | ACZ_REC_EXACT: the encoder process's processor affinity (as ACZ_REC_OBS_AFFINITY for OBS: the P-cores stay the game's) | `runtime/src/ps2_recexact.c:212` |
| `ACZ_REC_EXACT_BUFFERS` | `24` | `4`-`96` | ACZ_REC_EXACT: host buffers for the window captures (w x h x 1.5 bytes each: 5.3 MB at 2560x1440, 127 MB for 24), host-cached memory; they hold the frames while the encoder is behind (24 = 0.4 s, plus the pipe and the encoder's own queues) | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:30358` |
| `ACZ_REC_EXACT_WAIT` | `50` | ms | ACZ_REC_EXACT: with every buffer waiting for the encoder, the renderer waits this long for one (the video before the headset, the user's priority 2026-11-02) before it lets that capture go (the field repeats the previous image; counted as lost) | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:30360` |
| `ACZ_REC_EXACT_FRAMES` | off | N | [test] ACZ_REC_EXACT: the file ends (both inputs closed, the encoder waited for) after N frames while the game goes on: a complete file from a test run that is killed at its end | `runtime/src/ps2_recexact.c:95` |
| `ACZ_REC_CINE_TRACK` | off | file | [test] ACZ_REC_CINE: per game frame a CSV line -- field, seq, the head's and the camera's yaw / pitch / roll (deg), their angle apart, pulled in, the head's and the camera's position (mm), head-driven -- for plots of the smoothing (a private script) and as the session's recorded head track | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:29455` |
| `ACZ_REC_CINE_DUMP` | on | `0` | [test] ACZ_REC_CINE: the eye dumps (PS2_XR_DUMP_EYES) of a frame that draws the view also write the view's rectangle at the window's size (`eye_<field>_C.ppm`, bilinear blit) and the presented window itself (`eye_<field>_W.ppm`: what OBS records) | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:29454` |
| `ACZ_REC_CINE_DUMP_PAIR` | off | `1` | [test] ACZ_REC_CINE_SCALE: an eye-dumped frame's view (drawn at the view's own upscale) is drawn a second time at the eyes' density from the same camera (the same game frame, the smoothing standing still), and both rectangles are written as drawn, without resampling (`eye_<field>_P<upscale>.ppm`, e.g. `_P8` and `_P4`): the same-frame quality comparison (a private script); the window keeps the first | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:29451` |
| `ACZ_REC_CINE_DEBUG` | off | N | [debug] ACZ_REC_CINE: a line every N views (`rec-cine: N views`): the recording time on the renderer, the slots drawn privately, the head's angle off the camera (now, mean, max), how often it was pulled in, the share of draws left out (ACZ_REC_CINE_CULL) | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:29453` |
| `ACZ_REC_CLEAN_VERIFY` | off | N | [test] ACZ_REC_CLEAN: every N-th tail hashes the frame's host data the GPU reads (vertices, mesh records, indices, blocks, staged uploads) before and after it and logs a change (write-combined memory: tens of ms a hash, hence every N-th); eye dumps also write `eye_<field>_R_Rpost.ppm` (the headset's right image read again after the tail) | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:25730` |

## VR cockpit model

The 3D stereo cockpit that replaces the game's own in the R3 view (`runtime/src/ps2_video.c`, model built by `tools/cockpit/`). The game lighting and shadows come from the native renderer's light blocks, so they need `PS2_RN_VP=1`.

| Variable | Default | Values | Meaning | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_MODEL` | unset (game cockpit) | path to a `.cpit` | Replace the game's R3 cockpit with this model, drawn in true stereo with live instruments | `runtime/src/ps2_settings.c:1819`, `runtime/src/ps2_settings.c:1887`, `runtime/src/ps2_settings.c:2493` (+4 more) |
| `ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_BRIGHT` | `1` with the game light, `0.85` with the fixed light | float | Brightness of the model | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:10984` |
| `ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_AMBIENT` | `0.55` | float | Ambient light on the model | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:10985` |
| `ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_GAME_LIGHT` | `1` | `0`/`1` | Light the model with the game's sun and sky lights; `0` uses a fixed light | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:10986` |
| `ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_SPEC` | `0.3` | float | Scale of the materials' specular shine (glass: its own sharp lobe, ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_GLASS_SHINE) | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:10987` |
| `ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_SHADOWS` | on | `0` | Sun shadow map (2048x2048) over the front cockpit | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:8810` |
| `ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_KEY_LIGHT` | `sun` | `sun`, `game` | Which of the game's three model lights is the cockpit's key light (its sun shadow map, highlights, the cloud volume's exit glare): `sun` = the light nearest C1's sun (S1) among those with at least a quarter of the brightest one's colour, within 35 deg, sticky (the last key stays unless another is clearly nearer); without one (night, no C1) the strongest, sticky (10 %). `game` = the strongest every frame (as before 2026-10-23: in M2 the fixed camera light (0, .707, .707) and the sun have equal colours and the pick flipped ~every 2.4 s -- 31 times in 35 s of straight flight -- so the cockpit's shadows and highlights jumped between two directions 60 deg apart and its lower half changed brightness by up to 14 %; in M3 the fixed light (colour 1.18) beats the sun (1.14), so the shadows came from above-behind whatever the sun did; M1, M4 (F-5E), M11: the sun is the strongest anyway) | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:12900`, `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:34851` |
| `ACZ_DEBUG_COCKPIT_KEY` | off | N | [debug] Log the game's three lights (cockpit frame, colour, angle to C1's sun) and the key light chosen every N frames, with the changes of the key and of the strongest light (`cockpit key:`) | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:12902` |
| `ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_SCENE_LIGHT` | `1` | `0`..`1` (strength; ENHANCEMENTS > COCKPIT > LIGHT FROM THE WORLD: ON `1` / SUBTLE `0.5` / OFF `0`) | The 3D cockpit lit by the world the eye images show (ov_light 2026-10-25; ps2_video.c `sl_*`, cockpit.frag): just before the cockpit is drawn, 15 8x8 patches of the left eye's world image are read back, each with its world elevation, and averaged over `_S` into three bands -- the sky above (> 20 deg), the horizon (-15..20), the ground (< -15; a band's brightest patch left out: the sun, a flare) -- that keep their last value while the head looks elsewhere (the light does not follow the gaze). The model is then lit by a sky dome over the world's up -- an upward face takes (2 sky + horizon) / 3, a vertical one (sky + 2 horizon + ground) / 4, a downward one (2 ground + horizon) / 3, means in linear light, plus the sun's bounce inside the cockpit (`_BOUNCE`), occluded by ao^(1/2.2) (the bake's share of the sky, in linear light) -- instead of the game's ambient and its non-key model lights (a light from straight above in M7, below in M1 / M4 / M11, a fixed camera light in M2-M4: made for the aircraft seen from outside); the key light (the sun: its shadows and highlights, the cloud shadow, the in-cloud light, the flashes) stays as before, highlights from the key light only. Fades in over the first ~1 s of world seen; at night (M11) off (`_NIGHT`); bands forgotten after 2 s without a cockpit drawn. Tornado, same-frame A/B (PS2_PERF_AB_FLIP=scenelight,glassspec, light runs, governor 0, 7 head poses), the cockpit's mean linear luminance after / before: M7 storm x0.61-0.65 (the wing plate's brightest parts 84/113/131 -> 64/80/91, the sky 60/75/85), M12 x0.73, M6 x0.86, M4 x1.02, M3 x1.07, M5 x1.31 (M11 at night: the game's lights, `_NIGHT`), M1 overcast over snow x1.76; the F-1 in M4 x0.50. `0` = the game's model lights as before (ENHANCEMENTS OFF). GPU (exclusive 4x M7, governor 0, `_AB`=30, 2 runs x 3 windows): +0.006 +-0.027 ms per stereo frame, the noise floor (a first version that copied the whole light block per pixel: +0.030) | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:19129` |
| `ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_SCENE_LIGHT_S` | `1.5` | `0.05`..`30` s | Cockpit scene light: time constant of the sky / horizon / ground bands (a band seen in fewer of its 5 patches learns more slowly) | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:19133` |
| `ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_SCENE_LIGHT_BOUNCE` | `0.55` | `0`..`4` | Cockpit scene light: the sun's light thrown about inside the cockpit, added to every facing of the dome: this x the key light's colour x the sun's height (sin of its elevation) x the share of the sun getting through (our clouds' shadow at the aircraft, inside a cloud). It keeps a sunlit cockpit's shade as bright as the game's high clear-day ambient had it (Tornado, same-frame A/B: M3 x1.07, M4 x1.02 the cockpit's mean linear luminance; without it x0.68 / x0.58, the F-1 in M4 x0.24) and fades under an overcast, in a storm, at dawn / dusk (a low sun) and at night (a dim key light). `0` = the sky dome alone | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:19142` |
| `ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_SCENE_LIGHT_NIGHT` | `0` | `0`..`1` | Cockpit scene light at night (S5's night flag: M11): its strength there. `0` = the game's night lights stay -- they light the panel from below in the city's orange glow; the dome (an orange-brown murk, ~0.2) took the A-10's panel to ~0.74 of its brightness and contrast (display mean 17 -> 12.7, same-frame A/B) while the night cockpit already sat in its world (Tornado cockpit / world 0.22 before, 0.25 with it) | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:19139` |
| `ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_NIGHT_LIGHTS` | `1` | `0` (off)..`2` | (night_sky 2026-10-29, bug #197, the user 2026-10-29 01:15: "you also wanted to fix the instrument lighting at night") Night instrument lighting of the 3D cockpit at night (S5's night flag: M11; eased in over ~2 s): the panel's markings -- small light, unsaturated features on a darker ground (pointers, numerals, scales, integrally lit legends: the texel against its texture's 8x8-texel mean three mip levels up, so large light surfaces such as a seat frame or light grey paint do not count) -- glow in the light's tint (+0.50 x the texel, not occluded), and every surface of the panel area gets a faint flood (+0.10 x the texel x AO: black stays black). Panel area, from the head: below the eye by 6 cm or more, within 1.4 m, the main panel ahead (z < -0.3 m) and the side consoles (\|x\| > 0.22 m), not behind the eye -- not the seat, the canopy frame or the headrest; never the glass, the screens (self-lit already) or the world; the canopy's reflection is baked unlit, so no glare in the glass. F-15C, M11, panel box (same frame, PS2_PERF_AB_FLIP=nightpanel): mean 15 -> 24 levels, p90 25 -> 58 (first version). A strength: 1 = the level above. PS2_PERF_AB_FLIP=nightpanel: the same-frame A/B re-draw without it | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:14777` |
| `ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_NIGHT_LIGHTS_TINT` | `1.0,0.86,0.66` (a warm incandescent white) | `r,g,b` `0`..`1` | The night instrument lighting's colour (5 bits a channel), e.g. `1,0.35,0.25` red, `0.55,1,0.6` NVIS green | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:14781` |
| `ACZ_DEBUG_NIGHT_LIGHTS` | off | N | [debug] Every N-th cockpit frame: `cockpit: night lights level ...` (the eased night level and S5's night flag) | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:14787` |
| `ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_SCENE_LIGHT_GAIN` | `1` | `0.1`..`4` | Cockpit scene light: scale of the sky dome (1 = a white face open to the sky shows the sky's own brightness) | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:19136` |
| `ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_SCENE_LIGHT_AB` | off | frames N | [test] The cockpit scene light on / off (the patch copy too) in blocks of N frames, the mean GPU time per frame of each logged every 40 blocks (`scene-light-ab:`); `PS2_PERF_AB=1 PS2_PERF_AB_FLIP=scenelight` gives the exact same-frame image A/B | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:19145` |

| `ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_GLASS_SHINE` | `256` | shininess, `0` | The canopy / windscreen glass's own sun highlight (ov_light 2026-10-25, ov_visual's item 8: M10 looking up into the sun, a bright cone rising from the canopy bow): the converted materials give the glass a satin-rough Blinn lobe (specular 0.5, shininess 50-64; half power ~8 deg off the mirror direction) that a canopy curved about one axis smears into a streak tens of degrees long. The glass now takes this shininess (half power ~4 deg: half the angular size) at `_SPEC` of the old lobe's energy; it fades with the frame-time governor's glass lever (ACZ_VR_GOV_LADDER `glass`). `0` = the material's lobe as before. `PS2_PERF_AB_FLIP=glassspec`: same-frame A/B | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:15674` |
| `ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_GLASS_SPEC` | `0.25` | `0`..`10` | The glass highlight's energy as a share of the old lobe's (its strength x (shine + 1) / (material shininess + 1) x this): 0.25 keeps the old peak (a smaller, not brighter glint); 0.5 at 256 or 512 piled the light into a saturated white oval over the clouds (M10 A/B) | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:15677` |
| `ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_MSAA` | `4` (`2` with ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_DETAIL=low) | `1`, `2`, `4`, `8` (capped by the GPU; below 2 = off) | Multisampling of the model's own render target | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:11721` |
| `ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_INDEXED` | on | `0` | The model's vertices (the converter writes three per triangle) stored once per mesh and drawn through an index buffer: the same triangles in the same order, identical pixels (PS2_PERF_AB_FLIP=cpidx: 0 px in M3; M1 at its re-draw noise floor), the vertex shader runs ~3x less (f15c 1.39 M -> 0.43 M vertices); cockpit about -0.05 ms per stereo frame in M3 (F-15C), -0.01 in M1 (F-5E) | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:8584` |
| `ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_PREPASS` | on | `0` | Depth pre-pass for solid parts in the MSAA path (one shade per pixel) | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:15977` |
| `ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_CUT_PREPASS` | on | `0` | (nx_cpit 2026-10-25) The parts whose texture has cut-out texels (alpha test; ps2_video.c `tex_cut`: one texel under 128 puts every part on that texture there) get a depth pre-pass too -- same vertex shader (invariant position), the colour pass's alpha cut (`cockpit_cut.frag`, c.a < 0.5, the threshold cockpit.frag applies to every non-glass part, mirrors included) -- after the solid parts' one, then their colour where the depth is equal, like the solid parts. Before (`0`) they were drawn with the full shader, GEQUAL and depth write, after the solid parts' colour: every fragment of theirs in front of what was there was shaded, and the solids behind them first. Per sample the result is the same (the last fragment at the nearest depth wins either way, ties included): same-frame A/B `PS2_PERF_AB=1 PS2_PERF_AB_FLIP=cutpre` 0 px in all 64 game-frame eye pairs (Typhoon, MiG-21bis, F-1, Su-27, 8 head poses each), in-between frames at the `PS2_PERF_AB=2` re-draw floor (world pixels only). Triangles on that path: F-1 85 %, Typhoon 51 %, F-5E 44 %, YF-23A 40 %, MiG-21bis 31 %, MiG-29A 9 %, A-10A 8 %, M2000D 7 %, Su-27 3.5 %, ADF-11F 99 %, the others under 2 %. Saving per stereo frame (exclusive 4x in-run A/B, M4 sea, census head script, ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_AB item `nocutpre`, 136 pairs a run, 1-3 runs): YF-23A 0.165, F-5E 0.153, ADF-11F 0.117, F-1 0.107, MiG-21bis 0.049, M2000D 0.026, F-15C 0.022, MiG-29A 0.021, Su-27 0.019, Typhoon 0.018, A-10A 0.009 ms (+-0.007..0.021); no model slower | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:9006` |
| `ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_SHADER_SPLIT` | on | `0` | (fq_opt 2026-10-26) cockpit.frag specialised per pipeline (constant 0, CPIT_KIND): the solid parts' pipelines (the frame's, the multisampled pass's GEQUAL and EQUAL ones and their 2-sample twins) without the glass code, the glass pipelines without the solid-only code (shadow map taps, flashes, mirrors); the interior cube's bake and every other pipeline keep the whole shader. The same code runs for every part (the solid pipelines never draw glass, light.w >= 0 there; the glass ones only glass, light.w < 0); what goes is only code a pipeline can never take, and with it the register count the longest path (the canopy water) set for the whole übershader: pipeline statistics 95-96 registers (21 warps an SM) -> solid 56-57 (36 warps), glass 80 (25 warps). Same-frame A/B `PS2_PERF_AB=1 PS2_PERF_AB_FLIP=cpspec` with the time-driven parts off (our clouds, shader water, immersion, M12 also eye adaptation): game frames 0 px in M12 F-15C (7 frames, 14 eye images); M4 MiG-29A (eye adaptation on) 0 px in 7 of 10 views, two views 17-23 px by 1-3 levels scattered over the panel (the driver schedules the same arithmetic differently: the clean controls never differ in game frames), one view a whole-sky exposure step of the eye adaptation that did not recur in the repeat; in-between frames at their control floor; in-run A/B `PS2_PASS_AB=60 PS2_PASS_AB_WHAT=cpspec` (exclusive 4x, user settings) M4 MiG-29A -0.024 +- 0.011 ms per stereo frame (3 lines), M12 F-15C wet canopy -0.083 +- 0.012 (4 lines). `0` = one pipeline per state with the whole shader, as before | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:9019` |
| `ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_FRESH_HEAD` | on | `0` | (lb_f15 2026-10-26, playtest M3 F-15C 21:19 "this thing behind is blinking" while checking six) The 3D cockpit is drawn from the newest located eye position -- the pose predicted for the display time of the headset frame being recorded -- instead of the position the game frame was tagged with when the EE read it; the orientation stays the tagged one (the layer is submitted with it and the compositor turns the image to the display's orientation, which it cannot do for a moved eye: no depth, rotation only). The tagged position is a field or two old when the game frame reaches the headset and frames drawn between game frames (smooth mode) reuse it 8.3 ms later still, so with the head moving the nearest parts were drawn from where the eye had been and alternated at 60 Hz between two offsets: twisting round to check six swings the eyes ~0.1 m about the neck (0.2-0.3 m/s) next to the seat's headrest (5-15 cm away), where 3 mm is ~2 deg. The world is drawn as before (far: no visible parallax). Not used while the runtime gets depth (PS2_XR_DEPTH) or in alternate-eye stereo. Sim (M3 F-15C, a check-six head over the left shoulder moving 0.23 m/s and 50 deg/s): the eye had moved avg 5.1 / 5.6-5.9 mm (max 15 mm) since the game frame's pose in game / in-between frames; same frame re-drawn the old way (PS2_PERF_AB_FLIP=freshhead) the seat beside the head sits 10-45 px (scale 2) elsewhere, world pixels identical; consecutive presented frames (PS2_XR_DUMP_SEQ) of the old way hold the seat still in every in-between frame and jump 19-34 px in the next game frame, the new way moves it every frame. No GPU work added. `cockpit: fresh head` lines (a window of 1200 recorded frames, ~10 s): how far the eye had moved in game / in-between frames (the headset's real numbers). `0` = the game frame's position as before; PS2_PERF_AB_FLIP=freshhead = the same-frame re-draw the other way | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:15698` |
| `ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_MSAA_AUTO` | (none; `tris:430000` with ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_DETAIL=medium) | comma list of cockpit files (base names) and / or `tris:N` (every model of at least N triangles: a rule for any cockpit, perf4_cockpit 2026-11-03); the ini key `cockpit_edge`, F4 > Enhancements > Expert (ini): BEST (unset) / AUTO (`su27.cpit,f15c.cpit`) | (nx_cpit 2026-10-25) The cockpit models drawn at 2 samples while ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_MSAA asks for 4: their 2-sample twins are made (with the governor off too) and the governor's MSAA lever then acts on the other models only. The 4x -> 2x saving per stereo frame (exclusive 4x in-run A/B): M4 F-15C 0.188 +- 0.018, Su-27 0.153 +- 0.012 (2 runs each), A-10A / F-14D ~0.09, Typhoon 0.08, MiG-21bis 0.06, Tornado 0.00 (one run each, 5 items); M12 overcast F-15C 0.258 +- 0.021 (the wet canopy glass's blending). Not the default: the same frame at 2x (PS2_PERF_AB_FLIP=msaa2) steps the canopy frame's diagonals against the sky and breaks the instruments' thin needles and ticks (0.02-0.27 % of the pixels by more than 16 levels), and the frame-time governor already sheds it when the frame is over budget. The menu's AUTO is there for a headset look. (perf4_cockpit 2026-11-03) The heavy three, M4 sea, in-run A/B: MiG-31 (707k triangles) 0.223 +- 0.021, Rafale M (986k) 0.302 +- 0.023, F-117A (155k) 0.112 +- 0.025 ms (headset x1.75 ~0.39 / 0.53 / 0.20); MiG-31 same-frame 4x flip 0.33-0.41 % of the pixels by more than 16 levels, only on geometric edges (bezels, the canopy bow's diagonal; the dial text stays). `tris:430000` would take exactly the old pair (F-15C 506k, Su-27 431k) plus the MiG-31, the Rafale and the Mirage 2000D (505k), and any future model that dense | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:8929` |
| `ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_DETAIL` | `high` (unset) | `high`, `medium`, `low`; the ini key `cockpit_detail`, F4 > Enhancements > Expert (ini): HIGH (unset) / MEDIUM (`medium`) / LOW (`low`); the QUALITY PRESET sets it | (perf6_cockpitladder 2026-11-04) One quality step for the 3D cockpit, for weaker PCs. `high` = everything as before, byte for byte (no side file read, no texture halved, the 4x target). `medium` / `low` only change the built-in defaults of the levers below -- a lever's own switch (set by hand or by its saved ENHANCEMENTS value) still wins: mesh LOD level 1 / 2 (ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_LOD; side files from tools/cockpit/cpit_lod.c), 2x anti-aliasing for models of >= 430k triangles (ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_MSAA_AUTO=tris:430000) / for all (ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_MSAA=2), textures within 256 / 128 MB (ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_TEX_BUDGET). The sun shadow map, the scene light, the mirrors and the canopy glass are the same in every level. Measured (simulator on the 3090 Ti, exclusive 4x, the user's settings, ACZ_VR_GOVERNOR=0, PS2_BENCH_REPEAT=2, M4 low over the sea, in-run ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_AB items cdmed / cdlow in blocks of 30 frames, ms saved per drawing +- SE, 28-101 block pairs; the headset estimate is x1.75): medium / low MiG-31 0.411 +- 0.024 / 0.500 +- 0.052, Rafale M 0.147 +- 0.056 / 0.326 +- 0.062, F-117A 0.108 +- 0.059 / 0.163 +- 0.047, F-5E 0.105 +- 0.031 / 0.100 +- 0.031, J35J 0.069 +- 0.033 / 0.055 +- 0.028; every displayed frame saves it while the in-between frames draw the cockpit (ACZ_VR_SMOOTH_LAYER_COCKPIT=1: the game frames only). The cockpit's own memory (`MB on the GPU`, without the multisampled target) high / medium / low: MiG-31 545 / 310 / 186, Rafale M 491 / 336 / 208, F-117A 788 / 277 / 149, F-5E 184 / 185 / 153, J35J 115 / 115 / 115; low's 2-sample target also saves 117 MB at 4x against the 4-sample one (computed from the image sizes; 66 MB at 3x), while medium's 2-sample twin for a dense model adds that much unless the governor's MSAA lever has already made it. Same-frame 4x flips (PS2_PERF_AB_FLIP=cdmed / cdlow): pixels over 16 levels in the eye's headset crop medium 0.00-0.87 %, low 0.01-1.54 %, mostly on geometric edges, plus soft AO changes in recesses at low. Untested in the headset. Logged once per model: `cockpit detail: <file>: <level> ...` (LOD level and triangles drawn, samples, textures before -> after, MB on the GPU) | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:8917` |
| `ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_LOD` | unset (ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_DETAIL's: high `0`, medium `1`, low `2`) | `0`, `1`, `2` | (perf6_cockpitladder 2026-11-04) The 3D cockpit's mesh LOD level from its side file `<model>.lod` (ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_LOD_DIR, else next to the .cpit; tools/cockpit/cpit_lod.c). A level is, per part, an index list over the part's own vertices -- no vertex moves, no new vertex: first the converter's 5 cm AO subdivision undone where the baked AO allows (the merged triangle interpolates uv and normal exactly as the pair did; level 1 within 2/255 of the AO, level 2 within 8/255), then meshoptimizer's edge collapses within 0.5 / 1.5 px of the 4x eye target at the part's nearest approach (its bounding sphere minus a 15 cm lean, at least 0.2 m), normals, texture coordinates and AO held in the same error (level 1: 3/255 AO, 0.08 normal, 0.5 texel; level 2: 0.03, 0.2, 2 texels). Small detail goes first (rounded caps, knurls, bolt heads' segments, the AO mesh on flat panels); glass, the hands and the screens are never touched. Every pass draws the level (the sun's shadow map, the depth pre-pass and its colour, the depth into the frame) except the mirrors' real view and the canopy's interior cube. Triangles drawn: of as built, level 1 / level 2: MiG-31 707k: 58 / 43 %, Rafale M 986k: 88 / 81 %, F-117A 155k: 68 / 56 %, F-5E 176k: 54 / 39 %, J35J 70k: 82 / 75 %; all 43 cockpit files 9.85 M: 68 / 59 %. GPU saved per drawing (ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_AB lod1 / lod2, the same conditions as ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_DETAIL's numbers): MiG-31 0.269 +- 0.022 / 0.461 +- 0.027, Rafale M 0.084 +- 0.032 / 0.269 +- 0.070, F-117A 0.105 +- 0.070 / 0.192 +- 0.067, F-5E 0.117 +- 0.040 / 0.109 +- 0.032, J35J 0.005 +- 0.026 / 0.125 +- 0.087. The side file is 0.3-6 MB. Parts whose surfaces coincide with another part's within 0.05 mm keep the file's triangles at every level (the Rafale's split nodes: 36 parts, 474k triangles), since the GPU only sorts those by exact depth ties. Silhouette check (cpit_view -noglass, 5 views at the 4x target, 3.67 Mpx each): at most 99 pixels newly uncovered in a view. Only these five models were checked in the game's dumps; a model's first use at a lower level needs a look. No side file = the model as built (logged `no mesh LOD`); a side file of another build is refused (per-part triangle counts). A/B items `lod1` / `lod2`, PS2_PERF_AB_FLIP=`cplod1` / `cplod2` | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:10836` |
| `ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_LOD_DIR` | the .cpit's folder | folder | (perf6_cockpitladder 2026-11-04) Where ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_LOD reads `<model>.lod` (the .cpit's base name). Tests keep their side files out of the main tree's cockpits/ with it | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:10856` |
| `ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_TEX_BUDGET` | unset (ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_DETAIL's: high none, medium `256`, low `128`) | MiB; `0` = none | (perf6_cockpitladder 2026-11-04) The 3D cockpit's textures (with their mip chains; a format 4 BC texture is halved by skipping its stored top level, without re-encoding) kept within this many MiB on the GPU: at load the largest texture (texels; the first of equals) is halved (2x2 box on the CPU from the file's texels) until the set fits or every texture's longer side is down to 256 px. The cockpits are mostly magnified (perf4_cockpit's audit: 33-98 % of their textured pixels at lambda < 0), so a halved texture is softer where it is looked at closely; most of the 2048 px textures are 512 px sources upscaled x4, and 1024 still holds twice the source's texels. VRAM of the textures, 256 / 128 MB against as built: MiG-31 488 -> 248 / 124, Rafale M 416 -> 256 / 128, F-117A 766 -> 254 / 126, F-5E 160 -> 160 / 128, J35J 77 -> 77 / 77 MB; a model under the budget is untouched. GPU cost (the twins at 128 MB, ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_AB texb): MiG-31 +0.022 +- 0.047, F-117A +0.029 +- 0.053, F-5E -0.014 +- 0.046, J35J -0.001 +- 0.037 ms: nothing measurable | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:8936` |
| `ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_AB_TEX_BUDGET` | `256` (`128` with the A/B item `cdlow` alone) | MiB | [measurement] (perf6_cockpitladder 2026-11-04) The budget of the texture twins that the A/B items `texb` / `cdmed` / `cdlow` and PS2_PERF_AB_FLIP=`cptexb` sample: per texture the budget would halve, a view from mip level s of the full texture (the GPU's own mip: what the halved texture holds, within the box filter's rounding); no second copy in memory, made only when one of them asks | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:8947` |
| `ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_VRS` | `0` | `0`, `1` (`solid`), `2` (`glass`), `3` (`all`) | (perf4_cockpit 2026-11-03) Shading zones in the 3D cockpit's multisampled pass, the cockpit's counterpart of PS2_VRS_FOV=zones: away from each eye's optical axis the bit's parts are shaded once per 2x1 / 1x2 / 2x2 pixels (ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_VRS_ZONES); the depth, the coverage and every edge stay per sample. Bit `1`: the solid parts without cut-out texels (their colour pass where the depth pre-pass's depth is equal); bit `2`: the glass (canopy, windscreen, combiner, dial glass: its scratches, dust and drops coarser off-axis). The cut-out parts (alpha test), the HUD screens and the depth passes never change. Done by the primitive shading rate of cockpit_vrs.vert (per triangle, by its provoking vertex's angle off the eye's axis) combined by MAX with the pass's crop image (PS2_VRS_FOV), so no other pipeline changes; the device's primitiveFragmentShadingRate is enabled only when this (or its A/B, its flip) asks for it. Zones at 89 deg (all 1x1): the re-draw equals the plain pipeline up to the re-draw floor (1 px by 1 level of 0.9 M, as the PS2_PERF_AB=2 control). GPU per stereo frame (exclusive 4x, in-run ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_AB, class timers; MiG-31 / Rafale M / F-117A): the glass (`2`) M4 sea -0.144 +- 0.005 / -0.205 +- 0.008 / -0.136 +- 0.006 ms of its 0.37 / 0.48 / 0.37 (whole frame with the solid bit: -0.175 / -0.240 / -0.193 +- 0.02), M1 overcast with the canopy wet -0.258 +- 0.010 / -0.373 +- 0.015 / -0.241 +- 0.010 of 0.67 / 0.87 / 0.67 (whole frame -0.299 / -0.404 / -0.310 +- 0.02; headset x1.75 ~ -0.5 / -0.7 / -0.5); the solid bit only -0.023 / -0.046 / -0.053 of the model's 0.87 / 1.06 / 0.48 (whole frame -0.02 / -0.06 / -0.07 +- 0.02): the dense models are bound by their small triangles (with a 1-pixel scissor 0.40-0.41 ms of the pass are left), which give as many coarse invocations as fine ones -- not worth its look. In-between frames save the same. Look (4x same-frame flips, the left eye's crop): glass only, dry (MiG-31 M4): 1.3-2.9 % of the pixels differ at all, <= 0.003 % by more than 8 levels; glass only, wet (Rafale M1): 0.12 % by more than 16 levels, the drops' refraction rims beyond ~40 deg soft and blocky; the solid bit: panel labels and dial text 30-50 deg off the axis smeared (4.6-5.4 % of those pixels by more than 16 levels; the centre identical). Not a default: ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_VRS_DRY gives the glass the zones only while the canopy is dry (perf5_glasszones); bit 2 (glass) is the candidate for a headset look, bit 1 is not worth it; the desktop window, recordings (cine view) and eye dumps show the zones too | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:689` |
| `ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_VRS_ZONES` | `30,40` | `r1,r2` degrees off the eye's axis (0..89) | (perf4_cockpit 2026-11-03) The rings of ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_VRS: 1x1 inside r1, 2x1 / 1x2 between r1 and r2 (coarse along the radius, where the planar image has the most pixels per degree), 2x2 beyond r2 (at 4 samples the device's largest coarse fragment: 4x4 needs 64 coverage samples, the 3090 Ti takes 16). Wider rings keep more of the cockpit at full rate: the share of the shading work left is 43-46 % (solid) / 44-50 % (glass) at `30,40`, 51-55 / 50-62 % at `35,45`, 60-64 / 64-73 % at `40,50`, 73-75 / 78-84 % at `45,55` (tools/cockpit/cpit_view.c -zones, MiG-31 / Rafale / F-117A, ahead + panel views, the 4x target) | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:694` |
| `ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_VRS_DRY` | `0` (opt-in: a headset decision) | `1` | (perf5_glasszones 2026-11-03; the user agreed to shading zones on the dry canopy only) The glass's shading zones (ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_VRS bit 2's, rings ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_VRS_ZONES: 1x1 inside 30 deg off each eye's axis, 2x1 / 1x2 to 40, 2x2 beyond) only while nothing on the glass would show them; full rate otherwise. The gate shuts in the same frame as any of: water or frozen drops, mist, the condensation film, frost / rime (canopy_active()'s thresholds: below them nothing of it is drawn), a crack, a debris chip from a bit or a chunk (the crack layer: they stay for the mission), a debris fleck, or the sun lighting the glass's wear 10-85 deg off the view's axis (ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_VRS_DRY_SUN); it opens again after ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_VRS_DRY_HOLD (2 s) without any. Soot / dust, grime / smear and a kill's grit pits leave it open. The pipelines are made at the cockpit's load, with PS2_PERF_GLASS_VARIANTS each variant's zone twin (cockpit_vrs vertex stage + the gx fragment variant; zones + twin = zones + plain shader on the glass byte for byte, the flip `gxv`): the two savings stack and the switch costs nothing in a frame. Never in the recordings' cinematic view. `0`: no primitive shading rate, no twins, nothing new in the frame. Look (exclusive 4x same-frame flips, zones vs none, Rafale M over M4's sea, the left eye's crop, % of the pixels > 8 levels): dry glass 0.0007-0.0031 %, soot 0.45 0.0006-0.0019 %, grime + smear 0.0005-0.0022 %, grit pits 38-44 deg off the axis 0.0001-0.0008 %: inside perf4_cockpit's dry bound, yet side by side in 4x crops the smallest wear beyond 30 deg is softer (dust specks 2x2 blobs instead of crisp dots, a 1-px scratch bit fainter or gone): why it is not a default. What shuts it: drops 0.30-0.41 %, the last drops (water 0.03) 0.023-0.029 %, frozen drops + rime 0.26-0.33 %, X-02S M18 0.018-0.21 %, cracks 0.20 %, a fleck 0.0077 %, the film's diffused world blocky (> 2 levels on 0.33 %), the sun 38-41 deg off the axis 0.043-0.050 % (its lit scratch streaks blocky). GPU: exclusive 4x in-run ACZ_VR_COCKPIT_AB (item cvrsd = this gate, cvrsg = the zones always; PS2_GPU_CLASSES=2, PS2_BENCH_REPEAT=2, smooth layered, the census head script, no weapons), the glass class per frame against variants on, zones off, MiG-31 / F-117A over M4's dry sea: -0.050 +- 0.007 / -0.057 +- 0.008 ms (the gate open 81 % of those frames: the sun shut it while looking back up), -0.071 / -0.064 +- 0.004 while open (of 0.18 / 0.17); game and in-between frames alike, so -0.10 / -0.12 ms per 120 Hz pair (headset x1.75 ~ -0.18 / -0.20), -0.14 / -0.13 while open (~ -0.25 / -0.22). Rafale M: its run on the variants was disturbed (whole frame 11.0 ms against 8.3); against the plain shader -0.087 +- 0.010 / -0.121 +- 0.006, and the other two kept 0.8 of theirs on the variants: ~ -0.07 / -0.10, an estimate. The canopy wet (light rain) in the same runs: +0.007 / +0.010 +- 0.011-0.015: nothing. With ACZ_VR_SMOOTH_LAYER_COCKPIT=1 the in-between frames reuse a dry canopy's game-frame cockpit, so the saving counts in the game frames only. Log: `cockpit: dry-canopy gate open / shut ...` with the reason (the first 8 switches, then every 64th) and every 5 minutes its share of the frames. Untested in the headset | `runtime/src/ps2_video.c:662` |
